# 16. Hybrid cloud AI platform design
> **Track:** OpenShift AI Fundamentals — Corporate/L3
> **Audience:** Senior OpenShift administrators, platform engineers, MLOps engineers, SREs, security engineers, architects, and technical leads
> **Version basis:** Aligned to the Red Hat OpenShift AI Self-Managed 3.5 documentation stream available in July 2026. Always confirm support matrices, API stability, operator channels, and component status for your exact subscription and cluster release.
> **Hands-on project:** Design a two-site hybrid-cloud OpenShift AI architecture
---
## Executive definition
Hybrid-cloud AI design places data, training, inference, and governance across on-premises, public cloud, sovereign locations, and edge while preserving a consistent platform and controlled movement of artifacts.
This module treats the subject as an enterprise platform capability rather than a product-demo feature. The emphasis is architecture, ownership, controls, operability, failure handling, repeatability, and measurable production outcomes.
## Learning outcomes
- Create placement policies.
- Design artifact promotion.
- Plan cross-site operations.
- Define failure and recovery modes.
- Translate the topic into architecture diagrams, operational procedures, security controls, and acceptance tests.
- Explain trade-offs to application owners, data scientists, auditors, and senior management.
- Diagnose failures using a layer-by-layer and evidence-driven troubleshooting method.
## Scope and assumptions
- A supported Red Hat OpenShift cluster is available or planned.
- The OpenShift AI subscription, operator channel, and support matrix are validated before implementation.
- Identity, DNS, certificates, storage classes, ingress, registry access, and monitoring are treated as shared platform dependencies.
- GPU or alternative accelerator capacity is optional for fundamentals but mandatory for many production training and large-model inference workloads.
- S3-compatible object storage is planned for datasets, pipeline artifacts, model artifacts, and other lifecycle data when required.
- Production changes are delivered through Git, peer review, automated validation, and controlled promotion.
- Examples use placeholders and must not be copied into production without security review.
- Feature status can differ between General Availability, Technology Preview, Developer Preview, and community upstream implementations.
## Topic focus map
### Focus 1: Workload Placement
- Define what **workload placement** means for Hybrid cloud AI platform design.
- Identify the owner, consumer, supporting team, approval authority, and escalation path for workload placement.
- Document the normal flow, failure flow, recovery flow, evidence requirements, and version constraints for workload placement.
- Create measurable acceptance criteria for workload placement, including functionality, security, reliability, performance, and operability.
- Decide what is standardized centrally and what remains project-specific for workload placement.
- Review how workload placement changes between development, test, production, edge, hybrid-cloud, and disconnected environments.

## Component relationship map
### Component 1: on-prem OpenShift
- **Purpose:** Describe why on-prem OpenShift is relevant to Hybrid cloud AI platform design and which user or controller consumes it.
- **Inputs:** Identify configuration, credentials, data, artifacts, quotas, network access, and dependencies required by on-prem OpenShift.
- **Outputs:** Record APIs, artifacts, logs, metrics, status conditions, and user-visible outcomes produced by on-prem OpenShift.
- **Dependencies:** Map OpenShift APIs, storage, DNS, certificates, identity, Operators, and external services used by on-prem OpenShift.
- **Security:** Define service accounts, RBAC, secrets, network policy, image trust, audit, and data classification for on-prem OpenShift.
- **Reliability:** Define health checks, replica strategy, disruption controls, backup scope, recovery objective, and alerting for on-prem OpenShift.
- **Operations:** Establish installation, configuration, upgrade, troubleshooting, support, and decommission procedures for on-prem OpenShift.

## Enterprise architecture layers
### Layer 1: Business Outcome And Product Management
1. State the responsibility of the business outcome and product management layer for Hybrid cloud AI platform design.
2. Identify authoritative configuration sources for the business outcome and product management layer.
3. List upstream and downstream dependencies of the business outcome and product management layer.
4. Define isolation boundaries and data classification at the business outcome and product management layer.
5. Define SLIs for availability, latency, throughput, quality, saturation, and errors where applicable.
6. Define support ownership and escalation from project team to platform team to vendor.
7. Capture evidence through logs, events, metrics, traces, manifests, approvals, and test results.

## Reference manifests and command patterns

> These examples are instructional baselines. Validate API versions and component support on your exact OpenShift AI release.

### Project namespace

```yaml
apiVersion: project.openshift.io/v1
kind: Project
metadata:
  name: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/part-of: openshift-ai-fundamentals
    training.redhat.com/topic: "hybrid-cloud-ai-design"
    environment: lab
    owner: ai-platform-team
```

### ResourceQuota baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ResourceQuota
metadata:
  name: ai-team-quota
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  hard:
    requests.cpu: "8"
    requests.memory: 32Gi
    limits.cpu: "16"
    limits.memory: 64Gi
    requests.storage: 500Gi
    persistentvolumeclaims: "10"
    count/pods: "50"
```

### LimitRange baseline

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: LimitRange
metadata:
  name: ai-team-defaults
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  limits:
    - type: Container
      defaultRequest:
        cpu: "250m"
        memory: 512Mi
      default:
        cpu: "2"
        memory: 4Gi
```

### Default-deny NetworkPolicy

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: NetworkPolicy
metadata:
  name: default-deny
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
spec:
  podSelector: {}
  policyTypes:
    - Ingress
    - Egress
```

### ServiceAccount for controlled automation

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: ai-pipeline-runner
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
automountServiceAccountToken: true
```

### Role for project-level read operations

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: ai-project-reader
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
rules:
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "statefulsets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]
```

### RoleBinding

```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: ai-project-readers
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
subjects:
  - kind: Group
    name: ai-data-scientists
    apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: ai-project-reader
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

### S3-compatible data connection secret pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: lab-object-storage
  namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  labels:
    opendatahub.io/dashboard: "true"
    opendatahub.io/managed: "true"
  annotations:
    openshift.io/display-name: Lab object storage
type: Opaque
stringData:
  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: REPLACE_ME
  AWS_S3_ENDPOINT: https://s3.example.internal
  AWS_S3_BUCKET: ai-lab
  AWS_DEFAULT_REGION: us-east-1
```

### GitOps application pattern

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Application
metadata:
  name: hybrid-cloud-ai-design
  namespace: openshift-gitops
spec:
  project: default
  source:
    repoURL: https://git.example.internal/ai-platform/fundamentals.git
    targetRevision: main
    path: topics/hybrid-cloud-ai-design
  destination:
    server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
    namespace: ai-fundamentals-lab
  syncPolicy:
    automated:
      prune: true
      selfHeal: true
    syncOptions:
      - CreateNamespace=true
```

### Baseline validation commands

```bash
oc whoami
oc version
oc get clusterversion
oc get csv -A | grep -i -E 'rhods|opendatahub|nvidia|kueue|serverless|servicemesh'
oc get datasciencecluster -A -o wide
oc get dscinitialization -A -o wide
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-operator
oc get pods -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get routes -n redhat-ods-applications
oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp | tail -100
```
## From-scratch corporate project
### Project goal: Design a two-site hybrid-cloud OpenShift AI architecture
### Phase 1: Business framing and success criteria
**Objective:** Apply business framing and success criteria specifically to **Hybrid cloud AI platform design**.
1. Define the deliverable and an accountable owner for on-prem OpenShift, with explicit consideration of workload placement.
2. Record assumptions and unresolved questions for cloud OpenShift, with explicit consideration of data gravity.
3. Create objective entry and exit criteria for OpenShift AI, with explicit consideration of latency.
4. Identify dependencies and support boundaries for object storage replication, with explicit consideration of sovereignty.
5. Capture security and compliance requirements for model registry, with explicit consideration of connectivity.
6. Define evidence retained for audit for GitOps, with explicit consideration of artifact portability.
7. Write an automation or gitops approach for private connectivity, with explicit consideration of identity federation.
8. Define validation commands and expected results for identity, with explicit consideration of observability.
9. Document failure modes and rollback for central monitoring, with explicit consideration of DR.
10. Review capacity, cost, and licensing impact for edge inference, with explicit consideration of cost optimization.
11. Peer-review the design with platform and application teams for backup, with explicit consideration of workload placement.
12. Obtain change approval before production for policy, with explicit consideration of data gravity.
**Phase evidence:**
- Approved record for business framing and success criteria.
- Configuration or design artifact stored in Git for hybrid-cloud-ai-design.
- Test output showing both successful and intentionally failed paths.
- Named owner and escalation path for unresolved risk.

## Detailed hands-on lab sequence
### Lab 1: Collect cluster and operator inventory
**Goal:** Collect cluster and operator inventory for Hybrid cloud AI platform design, using on-prem OpenShift as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate workload placement, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
2. Perform step 2 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate data gravity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
3. Perform step 3 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate latency, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
4. Perform step 4 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate sovereignty, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
5. Perform step 5 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate connectivity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
6. Perform step 6 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate artifact portability, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
7. Perform step 7 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate identity federation, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
8. Perform step 8 for collect cluster and operator inventory; validate observability, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.
**Expected result:**
- The on-prem OpenShift state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to data gravity.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 2: Validate identity and group mappings
**Goal:** Validate identity and group mappings for Hybrid cloud AI platform design, using cloud OpenShift as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**
1. Perform step 1 for validate identity and group mappings; validate data gravity, capture command output, and compare actual state with the approved design.

- The cloud OpenShift state is observable and consistent with the declared configuration.
- Access is restricted to the intended identity and project boundary.
- Logs, events, metrics, and status conditions provide enough evidence for operations.
- Repeating the procedure produces the same result without undocumented manual changes.
**Failure injection:**
- Temporarily introduce a safe lab-only misconfiguration related to latency.
- Record the symptom, first failing layer, root cause, corrective action, and prevention control.
**Cleanup:**
- Restore the declared configuration from Git and verify reconciliation.
- Remove temporary credentials, test artifacts, and elevated access.
### Lab 3: Create a governed project namespace
**Goal:** Create a governed project namespace for Hybrid cloud AI platform design, using OpenShift AI as the primary observation point.
**Procedure:**

## Enterprise control catalogue
### Control 1: Identity And Access
- **Policy statement:** Hybrid cloud AI platform design must implement documented identity and access controls for on-prem OpenShift.
- **Design requirement:** The control must account for latency, tenant boundaries, and version-specific support constraints.
- **Implementation evidence:** Git commit, manifest, configuration export, approval record, and automated test result.
- **Operational evidence:** Metrics, logs, alerts, access review, capacity report, incident record, or restore test as applicable.
- **Failure criterion:** The control fails when unauthorized, untraceable, unsupported, unmonitored, or unrecoverable behavior is possible.
- **Review frequency:** Review after material platform changes and on the organization's scheduled control cycle.

## SRE and operational readiness
### Reliability objective 1: Dashboard Availability
- **Indicator:** Define how dashboard availability is measured for Hybrid cloud AI platform design.
- **Objective:** Select a target from business criticality, error budget, dependency reliability, and support capability.
- **Telemetry:** Identify source metrics, log queries, events, synthetic tests, and dashboard panels.
- **Alert:** Alert only when user impact or error-budget risk requires action.
- **Runbook:** Include diagnosis, mitigation, escalation, rollback, and post-recovery validation.
- **Review:** Evaluate trend, saturation, false positives, and improvement actions during service review.

## Troubleshooting playbook
### Scenario 1: Dashboard Is Unavailable
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Hybrid cloud AI platform design and is first observed around model registry.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Taint, toleration, affinity, or node-selector mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get pods -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get subscriptions.operators.coreos.com -A`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass data gravity controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.
### Scenario 2: User Cannot See A Project
- **Context:** The issue occurs while operating Hybrid cloud AI platform design and is first observed around edge inference.
- **Likely hypothesis:** Artifact metadata mismatch.
- **First command:** `oc get csv -A`
- **Second command:** `oc get events -A --sort-by=.lastTimestamp`
- **Diagnosis method:** Confirm scope, compare desired and actual state, inspect conditions, events, logs, metrics, dependencies, and recent changes.
- **Corrective action:** Repair the verified cause at the lowest responsible layer; do not bypass latency controls.
- **Validation:** Repeat the failed transaction, check status convergence, verify user-visible behavior, and observe for regression.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated precheck, policy, alert, capacity threshold, test, or documented ownership control.

## Production design decisions
### Decision 1: Dedicated Versus Shared Openshift Cluster
- **Question:** Which dedicated versus shared OpenShift cluster pattern best supports Hybrid cloud AI platform design?
- **Option A:** Optimizes centralized consistency, reuse, governance, and operational efficiency.
- **Option B:** Optimizes isolation, autonomy, locality, or specialized performance.
- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

- **Selection criteria:** Business criticality, data location, latency, scale, skills, security, availability, cost, and supportability.
- **Evidence:** Benchmark results, threat model, cost model, failure test, and stakeholder approval.
- **Revisit trigger:** Material workload growth, new regulation, new supported feature, incident trend, or architecture change.

## Expanded glossary
### Term 1: AI platform
- **Definition:** A managed set of services, controls, and workflows for creating and operating AI solutions.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate AI platform to on-prem OpenShift, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 2: MLOps
- **Definition:** Practices that automate and govern the machine-learning lifecycle.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate MLOps to cloud OpenShift, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 3: GenAIOps
- **Definition:** Operational practices for foundation-model, RAG, prompt, agent, evaluation, and inference lifecycles.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate GenAIOps to OpenShift AI, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 4: ModelOps
- **Definition:** Portfolio-wide governance and operations for models, versions, approvals, deployments, and risk.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate ModelOps to object storage replication, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 5: Workbench
- **Definition:** A project-scoped interactive development environment such as JupyterLab or code-server.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate Workbench to model registry, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 6: Pipeline
- **Definition:** A repeatable workflow of containerized tasks for data and model lifecycle automation.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate Pipeline to GitOps, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 7: Model registry
- **Definition:** A governed metadata and artifact catalog for model versions and lifecycle state.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate Model registry to private connectivity, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 8: Model catalog
- **Definition:** A curated discovery source for models that can be evaluated, registered, or deployed.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate Model catalog to identity, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 9: Model serving
- **Definition:** Runtime services that expose trained models through network APIs.
- **Relevance:** In Hybrid cloud AI platform design, relate Model serving to central monitoring, ownership, configuration, and user impact.
- **Operational check:** Confirm status, dependencies, permissions, telemetry, capacity, and recovery behavior.
- **Architecture question:** Decide whether this capability is centralized, federated, project-scoped, cluster-scoped, or externally managed.
### Term 10: Inference

## Final production-readiness statement
A production implementation of **Hybrid cloud AI platform design** is ready only when functionality, security, reliability, performance, supportability, cost, auditability, and recovery have objective evidence. A successful demonstration is not equivalent to an operationally supported enterprise service.
